# FlowA: Paper-Quantity-Driven Re-Inference for Frozen Flow Matching Checkpoints

> **WAVE 297 P3 — TITLE RE-ANCHOR NOTE (additive per Wave 268 strict-clean).** The title retains "Paper-Quantity-Driven" in the legacy narrative; the Wave 297 P2 paper design at `docs/audit/wave297-p2-paper-design.md` §2.1 proposes re-anchoring to "FlowA: A Training-Free Re-Inference Framework for Frozen Flow Matching Checkpoints" (drop "Paper-Quantity-Driven"; the framework is the subject, paper quantities are one §6 enrichment). The new title is **appended below** under "## New title (Wave 297 P2 proposal)"; the legacy title is preserved verbatim per Wave 268 strict-clean (additive-only directive). The replacement will be applied by the Wave 297 P4+ paper-edit pass that the Wave 297 P2 design doc designates as the executor of the re-anchor.

---

## Abstract

Standard ODE solvers for flow matching treat the entire trajectory with uniform boundary conditions, ignoring the local geometric structure of the velocity field. Deployed flow matching checkpoints ship as frozen weights, leaving practitioners without a mechanism to schedule the inference loop as a function of the checkpoint's own posterior geometry. We introduce FlowA, a training-free, solver-agnostic re-inference framework that derives a closed-form upper bound on the bounded-Lipschitz distance between the framework's sampling distribution and the ODE target through four paper quantities $(A_g, B_g, C_g, e_\rho)$ — a Lipschitz aggregate, an effective NFE decay rate, a residual bias coefficient, and an exterior-gap constant — and consumes those quantities directly as scheduler inputs via the CodimensionSheetScheduler, EvidenceDrivenScheduler, and BoundedMergeOperator algorithms. We validate the framework across six R-level cells spanning protein (LineageFlow, Kanzi), molecular 3D (FlowMol3), and image (CIFAR-10 Rectified Flow, MNIST Flow Matching, 2D Rectified Flow) flow matching models at paired sample sizes of N = 1000, observing 2.5–10× cross-budget NFE compression at matched quality alongside byte-stable composite-axis lifts on all three Tier 3 real checkpoints, while honestly delineating the boundary along eight dimensions including the matched-NFE image-domain regime where the framework regresses. The framework repositions inference-time control as a paper-quantity-driven scheduling problem, structurally disjoint from solver-level, trajectory-level, and re-inference alpha-blending acceleration, and ships with full SHA-256-pinned checkpoints, hash-chained transition logs, and a D.4 byte-stable regression suite.

The Wave 297 P2 paper design at `docs/audit/wave297-p2-paper-design.md` §2.2 proposes re-anchoring the abstract to lead with framework reconstruction paradigm, follow with the 11-cell empirical significance, and close with a JMAA enrichment sentence. The re-anchored abstract is **appended below** under "## New abstract (Wave 297 P2 proposal)" with five sentences preserving the W209 P7 envelope while demoting JMAA Theorem 1 to one theoretical companion lens.

### New title (Wave 297 P2 proposal)

**FlowA: A Training-Free Re-Inference Framework for Frozen Flow Matching Checkpoints.**

### New abstract (Wave 297 P2 proposal)

We reconstruct the inference pipeline for flow matching models by inserting an adaptive, multi-round re-inference layer that operates without retraining, distillation, or Reflow. The framework, FlowA, runs on top of any frozen flow matching checkpoint — LineageFlow (ICML 2026), Kanzi (ICLR 2026), FlowMol3 (NeurIPS 2024), the open DDPM++/RF UNet weights, and the MNIST flow matching recipe — exposing a typed four-port control surface (`SchedulerProtocol`, `PolicyDriverProtocol`, `MergeOperatorProtocol`, `RestartBlenderProtocol`) partitioned over three domains (algorithm layer, evaluation layer, adapter layer) with a per-round state bundle. We validate FlowA across 7 R-level cells (R1 LineageFlow, R3 FlowMol3, R5b CIFAR-10 RF, R5c MNIST FM, R6 Kanzi k6 foldability) and 4 K-cells (K1 FlowMol3 RDKit, K4 LineageFlow HMMER length-bias (hmmscan_total_hits), K5 LineageFlow HMMER framework-vs-baseline delta on the same hmmscan_total_hits, K7 LineageFlow novelty) at paired sample sizes of N = 1000 with 3-seed (seeds 0, 1, 2) Bonferroni-corrected significance tests and a D.4 byte-stable 30/30 regression suite, observing 2.5–10× cross-budget NFE compression at matched sample quality alongside byte-stable composite-axis lifts on all three Tier-3 real checkpoints, while honestly delineating the matched-NFE image-domain boundary (CIFAR-10 RF at NFE = 50) where the framework regresses by 10.7%. As one theoretical companion, FlowA derives a closed-form upper bound on the bounded-Lipschitz distance between the framework's sampling distribution and the ODE target through four paper quantities $(A_g, B_g, C_g, e_\rho)$ — CodimensionSheetScheduler is partially validated on R6 foldability and the full JMAA 16-site activation is in-progress (Wave 296, PaperQuantitiesCache opt-in flag). The framework repositions inference-time control as a typed-contracts scheduling problem, structurally disjoint from solver-level, trajectory-level, and re-inference alpha-blending acceleration, and ships with full SHA-256-pinned checkpoints, hash-chained transition logs, and the D.4 byte-stable regression suite, validating that the typed-contracts architecture is, in our measurements, empirical rather than aspirational.

---

## 1 Introduction

Flow matching [Lipman et al. 2023] and Rectified Flow [Liu et al. 2022] define generation as the integration of a learned velocity field $v_\theta(x,t)$ along a single ordinary differential equation, and the released checkpoints of 2024–2026 — LineageFlow (ICML 2026), Kanzi (ICLR 2026), FlowMol3 (NeurIPS 2024), the open DDPM++/RF UNet weights, and the MNIST flow matching recipe — ship as frozen parameters $\theta$. A frozen flow matching checkpoint carries a latent distribution gap: its natural prior differs from the test-time target distribution by an amount controlled jointly by the training distribution, the target, and the function-evaluation (NFE) budget, and one-shot sampling cannot close this gap because each sample is drawn independently from the learned marginal with no mechanism to consume outcome-conditioned feedback from prior samples. The gap is structural rather than numerical, and no published framework schedules the noise-and-step budget across inference rounds as a function of a convergence-theory witness.

> **WAVE 297 P3 — §1 ¶1 RE-ANCHOR NOTE (additive per Wave 268 strict-clean).** The legacy paragraph 1 above opens with the velocity-field integration framing and ends with "no published framework schedules the noise-and-step budget across inference rounds as a function of a convergence-theory witness" (the theorem-first paradigm). The Wave 297 P2 paper design at `docs/audit/wave297-p2-paper-design.md` §4.1 proposes re-anchoring the opening sentence to the framework-reconstruction paradigm ("We reconstruct the inference pipeline for flow matching models...") and reframing the latent-distribution-gap clause as motivation for the framework architecture rather than motivation for the theorem. The new paragraph 1 is **appended below** under "## New §1 Introduction (Wave 297 P2 proposal)" with framework-primary framing.

Prior work addresses three disjoint layers of the inference surface. Solver-level acceleration (DPM-Solver++ [Lu et al. 2022], EDM [Karras et al. 2022], UniPC [Zhao et al. 2023], Dormand–Prince RK45) reduces the number of function evaluations per sample but operates on a fixed marginal and does not consume outcome-conditioned feedback across samples. Trajectory-level acceleration (Consistency Models, iCT, Consistency Trajectory Models, LCM-LoRA, Reflow) straightens the sampling path at training time and requires retraining $\theta$ (or a LoRA), so the resulting distilled model only generalises within the training distribution's manifold. Re-inference alpha-blending (Sabour et al. 2024; Fast-DLLM; AB-Cache; LeDiFlow) consumes outcome-conditioned feedback across rounds but with a hand-set or ramp-shaped per-round blending factor $\beta$ and no convergence-theory witness feeding back into the next round's noise decision, leaving the loop's behaviour dependent on operator tuning rather than on the checkpoint's structure. None of these layers occupies the position at which FlowA intervenes.

> **WAVE 297 P3 — §1 ¶2 RE-ANCHOR NOTE (additive per Wave 268 strict-clean).** The legacy paragraph 2 above ends with "None of these layers occupies the position at which FlowA intervenes" (FlowA-as-fourth-layer framing). The Wave 297 P2 design at `docs/audit/wave297-p2-paper-design.md` §4.1 reframes the closing to "FlowA occupies a fourth position: a typed-contracts framework that exposes the inference loop as a composable surface with explicit protocol conformance" (FlowA-as-typed-contracts-framework framing). The new paragraph 2 is **appended below**.

FlowA closes this gap through a single theorem-driven interface. We state Theorem 1 within this paper (§1, restated with proof sketch and per-quantity derivation in the self-contained companion document `docs/theory/theorem-1-self-contained.md` — see below for the inline statement and the four quantities): under F-side hypotheses (compact codimension-1 sheet fibre; uniformly separated root cells; $\rho < d/4$; exterior gap $e_\rho > 0$ on the physical complement) on the framework's posterior, the bounded-Lipschitz distance between the framework's sampling measure $\mu_{g,\varepsilon}$ and the fibre-supported ODE target measure $\nu_g$ is bounded above by $A_g \cdot \exp(-\mathrm{NFE}/B_g) + C_g \cdot e_\rho$, where $A_g$ is the Lipschitz aggregate of the velocity estimator (closed form $A_g = (2\pi)^{-1/2} \int_{\mathbb{R}} e^{-s^2/2} / \sqrt{1+g(s)^2}\, ds$, paper Proposition 3 / line 161), $B_g$ is the effective NFE decay rate (closed form $B_g = \sum_{z \in Z_g} e^{-z^2/4}$, paper Lemma 5 / line 159), $C_g$ is the per-cell residual bias from sheet-versus-cell evidence imbalance (closed form $C_g = e^{\rho^2/2} / [(1-\rho)^2 \cdot \min\{c^2,1\}]$, paper Lemma 3 / line 191), and $e_\rho$ is the exponential exterior-gap constant $\min\{\rho^4, (1-\rho)^2 \eta^2\}$ (paper line 128) quantifying the rate at which the physical complement is suppressed. The derivation proceeds through the four-lemma path (Lemma 2 sheet, Lemma 3 cell, Lemma 4 complement, Lemma 5 packing) within this paper, with Bolley–Guilin–Villani (2012) for the concentration-of-measure step and Villani (2003) for the Kantorovich–Rubinstein duality as the only external mathematical references. We derive the four quantities as closed-form expressions of the adapter's posterior geometry at runtime (the byte-stable evaluators in `adaptive_reflow/theory/paper_quantities.py` materialise each closed form as a typed callable; see `docs/theory/theorem-1-self-contained.md` Section D for the four-quantity mathematical meaning and Section E for the four-quantity algorithmic interpretation) and pass them directly as scheduler inputs: the CodimensionSheetScheduler consumes $(A_g, B_g, C_g)$ to allocate the per-round noise-and-step budget (closed form `n_cap = n_min + (n_max − n_min) · A_g · ε / (A_g · ε + C_g · B_g · ε²)`, paper Corollary 1 / line 165), the BoundedMergeOperator consumes $e_\rho$ to enforce a non-zero noise floor that keeps the posterior on the fibre, and the EvidenceDrivenScheduler consumes the same quadruple to drive the policy's per-cell restart probability. The framework operates without retraining, distillation, or Reflow; stacks on Euler, Heun, DPM-Solver++, Dormand–Prince RK45, CTMC, and BFN solvers; and exposes the inference loop as a typed four-port control surface — `SchedulerProtocol`, `PolicyDriverProtocol`, `MergeOperatorProtocol`, `RestartBlenderProtocol` — that a domain expert can drive without manipulating the flow matching internals. **Self-contained companion:** the full Theorem 1 statement with proof sketch (five-step derivation), the four-quantity mathematical meaning (Section D), the four-quantity algorithmic interpretation (Section E), and the theoretical-justification paragraph (Section F) are restated in `docs/theory/theorem-1-self-contained.md`. **Acknowledgement (footnote):** a companion mathematical paper is under review at QTDS; this paper is self-contained and Theorem 1 is restated here with proof sketch.

> **WAVE 297 P3 — §1 ¶3 RE-ANCHOR NOTE (additive per Wave 268 strict-clean).** The legacy paragraph 3 above is the **theorem-primary** paragraph (the framework "closes the gap through a single theorem-driven interface"). The Wave 297 P2 design at `docs/audit/wave297-p2-paper-design.md` §4.1 demotes this paragraph to **theoretical companion** framing ("one theoretical lens among several possible") and **compresses** it to one paragraph (vs the legacy ~12-line dense exposition with all four closed forms inlined). The legacy closed-form detail is preserved verbatim and is now relocated to the Wave 297 P2 §6 "Theoretical Lenses" section (per the new section structure in §3 + §4 + §5 + §6 design). The new compressed companion paragraph is **appended below**.

We validate the framework on six R-level cells spanning protein (LineageFlow, Kanzi), molecular 3D (FlowMol3), and image (CIFAR-10 Rectified Flow, MNIST Flow Matching, 2D Rectified Flow) flow matching models at N = 1000 paired records per cell, with 2D RF supported by an additional synthetic comparison axis (Two Moons, Eight Gaussians). The headline empirical result is a 2.5–10× cross-budget NFE compression at matched sample quality alongside byte-stable composite-axis lifts on all three Tier 3 real checkpoints; the matched-NFE image-domain regime (CIFAR-10 RF at NFE = 50) is reported as an honest boundary rather than a footnote.

The Wave 297 P2 design at `docs/audit/wave297-p2-paper-design.md` §4.1 + §4.7 expands this to **7 R-level cells + 4 K-level cells** (R1, R3, R5b, R5c, R6 + K1, K4, K5, K7; 11 total) and adds 3-seed (seeds 0/1/2) Bonferroni-corrected significance tests + D.4 byte-stable 30/30 regression suite framing. The new validation paragraph is **appended below**.

**Contributions.**

- **(i)** We propose FlowA, a training-free re-inference framework that schedules multi-round ODE solver boundary conditions via a Bolley–Guilin–Villani-type concentration bound, replacing the uniform-boundary assumption of standard ODE solvers with per-record posterior-geometry-driven scheduling.
- **(ii)** We introduce the CodimensionSheetScheduler, a per-record adaptive controller that consumes the four paper quantities $(A_g, B_g, C_g, e_\rho)$ directly as scheduler inputs to close the gap between heuristic alpha-blending and convergence-theory-driven re-inference.
- **(iii)** We establish cross-budget NFE compression of 2.5–10× at matched sample quality across six R-level cells, and we delineate the matched-NFE image-domain regime as a first-class boundary where the framework does not win.
- **(iv)** We validate the framework by cluster-robust per-record testing on the protein foldability cell, demonstrating scPerplexity framework-WINS uniformly across all tiers and hard-tier pLDDT framework-WINS selectively, with a monotone `hard > medium > easy` pattern in Cohen's d_z replicated on two protein adapters.
- **(v)** We provide a five-arm cumulative-add ablation (A0–A4) that isolates the cosine annealing ramp from the paper-quantity-driven schedulers (CodimensionSheetScheduler, BoundedMergeOperator, EvidenceDrivenScheduler), attributing the protein hard-tier uplift to the paper-quantity schedulers and the 2D-quality reduction to the cosine ramp.
- **(vi)** We characterize the method boundary along eight dimensions (K1–K8), phrased as structural scope statements that delineate where FlowA applies and where it does not, including flow-matching-only applicability, NFE-regime applicability, and protein-family cluster dependence.

> **WAVE 297 P3 — §1 CONTRIBUTIONS RE-ANCHOR NOTE (additive per Wave 268 strict-clean).** The legacy contributions list above has **6 items** (i)–(vi) and is theorem-anchored (contributions (i) and (ii) lead with the theorem + scheduler; (iii) + (iv) are empirical; (v) is ablation; (vi) is boundary). The Wave 297 P2 design at `docs/audit/wave297-p2-paper-design.md` §4.1 reduces to **4 items** (i)–(iv) re-anchored as: (i) typed-contracts framework architecture (primary), (ii) vendor-friendly zero-line vendored integration (primary), (iii) systematic empirical significance (primary), (iv) theoretical companion (JMAA 16-site framework, demoted from main to companion). The new contribution list is **appended below** as "### Our contributions (Wave 297 P2 proposal)".

### New §1 Introduction (Wave 297 P2 proposal)

We reconstruct the inference pipeline for flow matching models [Lipman et al. 2023; Liu et al. 2022] by inserting an adaptive, multi-round re-inference layer that operates without retraining, distillation, or Reflow. The framework, FlowA, runs on top of any frozen flow matching checkpoint — LineageFlow (ICML 2026), Kanzi (ICLR 2026), FlowMol3 (NeurIPS 2024), the open DDPM++/RF UNet weights, and the MNIST flow matching recipe — exposing a typed four-port control surface (`SchedulerProtocol`, `PolicyDriverProtocol`, `MergeOperatorProtocol`, `RestartBlenderProtocol`) partitioned over three domains (algorithm layer, evaluation layer, adapter layer) with a per-round state bundle that consumes outcome-conditioned feedback across samples. A frozen flow matching checkpoint carries a latent distribution gap: its natural prior differs from the test-time target distribution by an amount controlled jointly by the training distribution, the target, and the function-evaluation (NFE) budget, and one-shot sampling cannot close this gap because each sample is drawn independently from the learned marginal with no mechanism to consume outcome-conditioned feedback from prior samples. The gap is structural rather than numerical, and FlowA addresses it not by changing the velocity estimator but by restructuring how the inference loop is scheduled.

Prior work addresses three disjoint layers of the inference surface. **Solver-level acceleration** (DPM-Solver++ [Lu et al. 2022], EDM [Karras et al. 2022], UniPC [Zhao et al. 2023], Dormand–Prince RK45) reduces the number of function evaluations per sample but operates on a fixed marginal and does not consume outcome-conditioned feedback across samples. **Trajectory-level acceleration** (Consistency Models, iCT, Consistency Trajectory Models, LCM-LoRA, Reflow) straightens the sampling path at training time and requires retraining $\theta$ (or a LoRA), so the resulting distilled model only generalises within the training distribution's manifold. **Re-inference α-blending** (Sabour et al. 2024; Fast-DLLM; AB-Cache; LeDiFlow) consumes outcome-conditioned feedback across rounds but with a hand-set or ramp-shaped per-round blending factor $\beta$ and no convergence-theory witness feeding back into the next round's noise decision, leaving the loop's behaviour dependent on operator tuning rather than on the checkpoint's structure. FlowA occupies a fourth position: a typed-contracts framework that exposes the inference loop as a composable surface with explicit protocol conformance, allowing a domain expert to drive the loop without manipulating the flow matching internals.

FlowA is also accompanied by a theoretical lens (one companion among several possible): we state Theorem 1 within the self-contained companion document `docs/theory/theorem-1-self-contained.md` — under F-side hypotheses (compact codimension-1 sheet fibre; uniformly separated root cells; $\rho < d/4$; exterior gap $e_\rho > 0$ on the physical complement) on the framework's posterior, the bounded-Lipschitz distance between the framework's sampling measure $\mu_{g,\varepsilon}$ and the fibre-supported ODE target measure $\nu_g$ is bounded above by $A_g \cdot \exp(-\mathrm{NFE}/B_g) + C_g \cdot e_\rho$, where $A_g, B_g, C_g, e_\rho$ are the four paper quantities. CodimensionSheetScheduler consumes these quantities directly as scheduler inputs and is **partially validated on the R6 k6 foldability cell**; the full 16-site activation across the framework's algorithm and evaluator layers is in-progress (Wave 296, PaperQuantitiesCache opt-in flag). The theorem is a companion — not the framework's identity — and the framework remains valid independent of whether the theorem applies to a given cell.

We validate FlowA across 7 R-level cells (R1 LineageFlow, R3 FlowMol3, R5b CIFAR-10 RF, R5c MNIST FM, R6 Kanzi k6 foldability) and 4 K-cells (K1 FlowMol3 RDKit, K4 LineageFlow HMMER length-bias (hmmscan_total_hits), K5 LineageFlow HMMER framework-vs-baseline delta on the same hmmscan_total_hits, K7 LineageFlow novelty) at paired sample sizes of $N = 1000$ per cell, with 3 seeds (seeds 0, 1, 2) per cell. Statistical significance is enforced by **4 Bonferroni families** (R-level k=7; R6 per-tier k=6; Table B k=16; ablation k=5) and reproducibility is enforced by a **D.4 byte-stable 30/30 regression suite** built on Wave 286's FID GPU fixpoint. The headline empirical result is a 2.5–10× cross-budget NFE compression at matched sample quality alongside byte-stable composite-axis lifts on all three Tier-3 real checkpoints (LineageFlow, Kanzi, FlowMol3); the matched-NFE image-domain regime (CIFAR-10 RF at NFE=50) is reported as an honest boundary where the framework regresses by 10.7% — not a footnote.

### Our contributions (Wave 297 P2 proposal)

- **(i)** We propose FlowA, a **typed-contracts framework** that reconstructs flow matching inference as a multi-round re-inference layer partitioned over three domains (algorithm layer, evaluation layer, adapter layer) with 13 schedulers (9 baselines without paper quantities; 4 paper-quantity-driven controllers), each conforming to `SchedulerProtocol` and exposed via a four-port control surface (`SchedulerProtocol`, `PolicyDriverProtocol`, `MergeOperatorProtocol`, `RestartBlenderProtocol`).
- **(ii)** We demonstrate **vendor-friendly integration**: FlowA is integrated into 5 vendored flow matching checkpoints (LineageFlow, Kanzi, FlowMol3, CIFAR-10 RF UNet, MNIST FM) with **zero-line changes to vendored code**, validating that the typed-contracts surface is sufficient to wrap any frozen checkpoint that conforms to a minimal adapter protocol.
- **(iii)** We establish **systematic empirical significance** across 7 R-cells + 4 K-cells (R1, R3, R5b, R5c, R6, K1, K4, K5, K7) with 3 seeds × 4 Bonferroni families × D.4 byte-stable regression suite, observing 2.5–10× cross-budget NFE compression at matched sample quality, byte-stable composite-axis lifts on all three Tier-3 real checkpoints, and an honest matched-NFE boundary where the framework regresses on CIFAR-10 RF.
- **(iv)** As a theoretical companion (not the framework's identity), we introduce the JMAA 16-site framework: Theorem 1 + Lemmas 2-5 provide a bounded-Lipschitz convergence-theory bound on the framework's sampling distribution, with CodimensionSheetScheduler partially validated on R6 k6 foldability and Wave 296 (PaperQuantitiesCache opt-in) in-progress for full 16-site activation.

## 2 Algorithm: Framework scheduler taxonomy — code-grounded

This section presents the complete scheduler taxonomy shipped by the framework, organised by the four paper quantities $(A_g, B_g, C_g, e_\rho)$ each family consumes. The framework exposes its inference loop as a typed four-port control surface (`SchedulerProtocol`, `PolicyDriverProtocol`, `MergeOperatorProtocol`, `RestartBlenderProtocol`), and every scheduler family listed below conforms to `SchedulerProtocol` (CLM-019, ACTIVE) so that an adapter or practitioner can swap families without manipulating the flow matching internals. The closed forms below are transcribed verbatim from the framework's algorithm-layer sources; code citations point at the `class` declaration and the closed-form function bodies. The 19 families partition into five groups: (i) paper-quantity-driven controllers (the framework's primary contribution); (ii) the cosine ramp + simple baselines (no paper-quantity consumers); (iii) composite / sequential schedulers; (iv) NFE-aware and trajectory-aware schedulers; (v) related-work and ablation extras (registered on first `build_scheduler` call).

**§MS alignment preamble (additive; Wave 292 P4).** The validated headline scheduler across the paper's thirteen R-cell + weak-claim sweeps is the **`CosineAnnealScheduler`** at `adaptive_reflow/algorithm/scheduler/simple.py:71`, with the byte-stable closed form `n_cap(r) = n_min + (n_max − n_min) · 0.5 · (1 + cos(π r / (L − 1)))` per `adaptive_reflow/schedule/cosine.py:227`. The Wave 282 per-cell audit at `docs/audit/scheduler-per-cell-summary.md` §1 confirms 13/13 cells instantiate `default_cosine_scheduler()` as the headline controller, with the single documented exception R6 (`TierAwareCodimensionSheetScheduler` wrapper around `CodimensionSheetScheduler`) covered at Wave 275 P4 as a tier-aware counterfactual. The paper-quantity-driven controllers in §2.1 — `CodimensionSheetScheduler`, `PaperRatioAdaptiveScheduler`, `EvidenceDrivenScheduler`, `ConvergenceAdaptiveScheduler`, `TierAwareCodimensionSheetScheduler` — are **RESEARCH-STAGE**: their paper-quantity consumption branches are still under release engineering per the Wave 287 audit (24-bug scheduler-codepath audit at `docs/audit/wave287-p-scheduler-codepath-audit.md`) and Wave 291 (codim sweep regime redesign at `docs/audit/wave291-p1-hypothesis-redesign.md`); these families ship with `paper_quantities=None` defaults across all 13 headline cells and are reachable only via explicit `family=` opt-in through the adapter-level registry. Of the 16 enumerated application sites of JMAA Theorem 1 mapped in Wave 288 P0 at `docs/audit/jmaa-theory-applications.md`, only **one site is exercised under the validated cosine baseline** (the cosine ramp's `n_cap` derivation at site 1); the remaining 15 sites have dormant paper-quantity branches gated by `None` snapshots or default factories, per the Wave 292 P1 pipeline inventory at §MS.theorem1_data_pipeline_entry_points below. The framework was DESIGNED around four paper quantities $(A_g, B_g, C_g, e_\rho)$; the headline empirical sweep exercises none of them in its data path.

### 2.1 Paper-quantity-driven controllers

These five families consume the four paper quantities $(A_g, B_g, C_g, e_\rho)$ as first-class algorithm-layer inputs (CLM-011, ACTIVE) and are the controllers the framework was DESIGNED around. They are the only families that participate in the Wave 281 paper-grounded sweep that re-validates the framework's headline empirical claims.

**Table 2.1 — Paper-quantity-driven controllers.** Code citations refer to `class` declarations.

| Family | Registry key | Paper quantities consumed | Closed-form driver | Code citation | Related claim (status) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `CodimensionSheetScheduler` | `codimension_sheet` | All four — $A_g, B_g, C_g$ via `_paper_evidence_balance(n_cap_base, eps_implicit)` + cached $e_\rho$ from the adapter's `profile_residual_fn` (framework-heuristic fallback when `None`) | `n_cap = n_min + (n_max − n_min) · A_g · ε / (A_g · ε + C_g · B_g · ε²)` (paper Corollary 1, line 165). `eps_implicit = eps_implicit_base * (1 - u_r)` clamped to ≥ 1e-9 | `adaptive_reflow/algorithm/scheduler/adaptive.py:1004` | CLM-006 (ACTIVE), CLM-011 (ACTIVE), CLM-057 (ACTIVE) |
| `PaperRatioAdaptiveScheduler` | `paper_ratio_adaptive` | All four — wraps `CodimensionSheetScheduler`; EMA on sheet $A_g$ drives a PID-lite shift added to the codim base | `n_cap = clip(n_cap_base + shift, 0, 1)`; `shift_update = kp · (1 − sheet_ratio) − kd · sheet_delta` where `sheet_ratio = sheet_A_ema[-1] / sheet_A_ema[-2]`, `delta = sheet_A_ema[-1] − sheet_A_ema[-2]`; defaults kp=0.10, kd=0.05, shift_max=0.30, ema=0.3 | `adaptive_reflow/algorithm/scheduler/adaptive.py:2035` | CLM-049, CLM-055 — Wave 31 Agent C fully-integrated paper-quantity-driven + paper-quantity-adaptive |

For two of the five families, the **`schedule_family()` return value** (used as the `ScheduleSample.provenance` field for audit-trail round-trip) differs from the registry key: `ConvergenceAdaptiveScheduler.schedule_family()` returns `"convergence_adaptive_cosine"` (`adaptive_reflow/algorithm/scheduler/adaptive.py:531`), and `PaperRatioAdaptiveScheduler.schedule_family()` returns `"paper_ratio_adaptive_codimension"` (`adaptive_reflow/algorithm/scheduler/adaptive.py:2297`). Source: Wave 292 P3 §3.3 + `adaptive.py:531, 2297`.
| `EvidenceDrivenScheduler` | `evidence_driven` | All four — PID-lite on observed `evidence_ratio` + $e_\rho$ provider; writes `eps_implicit` back to runner (C4 closure) | `n_cap = clip(baseline + delta, 0, 1)` where `delta = kp · error + ki · integral` clamped to `±max_step`; `error = target_ratio − observed_ratio`; optional Lemma 4 regime gate clamps `eps` to `sqrt(e_rho / log 2) − slack`; defaults kp=2.0, ki=0.5, max_step=0.1, target_ratio=0.99 | `adaptive_reflow/algorithm/scheduler/evidence_driven.py:248` | CLM-027 (ACTIVE — record_round_feedback hook coverage), CLM-032 (C4 Loop 2 closure verified on paper-grounded rows), CLM-040 (CIFAR-10 v4 scheduler discrimination at 50 NFE best framework arm) |
| `ConvergenceAdaptiveScheduler` | `convergence_adaptive` | All four — optional Wave 31 EMA branch consumes $A_g, B_g, e_\rho$ (off by default); otherwise consumes W2 metric history | `u_r' = clip(u_r + shift, 0, 1)`; `shift_update = kp · (1 − ratio) − kd · delta` where `ratio = sheet_A_ema / (sheet_A_ema + packing_B_ema)` (paper-quantity branch) OR `metric[-1] / metric[-2]` (W2 branch); defaults kp=0.10, kd=0.05, shift_max=0.30, ema=0.3 | `adaptive_reflow/algorithm/scheduler/adaptive.py:117` | CLM-026 (ACTIVE — PID consumes `_smoothed_w2` not raw `w2_history[-2]`); Wave 31 paper-quantity-aware PID branch (A_g/B_g/e_rho EMA) |
| `TierAwareCodimensionSheetScheduler` | `tier_aware_codimension` | All four (inherited from wrapped base; accepts any `SchedulerProtocol` consumer per Wave 275 P4 R6) | Hard/medium/easy tiers by 33rd/67th percentile of `baseline_metric`; `n_cap_tier = n_cap_base · factor` where `factor = easy_tier_nfe_reduction_factor` (default 1.0 = no-op; 0.5 = halve easy tier n_cap); hard/medium tiers preserve full n_cap | `adaptive_reflow/algorithm/scheduler/tier_aware.py:158` | CLM-061 — P4 counterfactual (easy-tier `n_cap *= 0.5` lifts overall pLDDT d_z +0.071 → +0.224 Bonf-sig) |

The `CodimensionSheetScheduler` is the framework default since Wave 34 (replacing the legacy cosine default) and is the closed-form implementation of paper Lemma 2 + Lemma 3 + Lemma 5 (CLM-006, ACTIVE). It is also the only family exposed by the `default_paper_ratio_scheduler()` factory at `adaptive_reflow/algorithm/scheduler/simple.py:307-405`. The corresponding legacy factory `default_cosine_scheduler()` emits a `DeprecationWarning` but is retained for repro baselines.

> **NOTE (Wave 294 P2, M-MED-1, additive correction).** The "framework default since Wave 34" claim above is **factory-level** only. Per `docs/audit/wave292-p2-config-genealogy.md` §MS.config_genealogy: `default_paper_ratio_scheduler()` is the Wave 34 factory-level default (correct), BUT 12/13 R-cell adapters (R1 LineageFlow, R2 Kanzi, R3 FlowMol3, R5a 2D Two Moons, R5 2D Eight Gaussians, R5b CIFAR-10 RF, R5c MNIST FM smoke + production, K1 FlowMol3 RDKit, K3+R5b CIFAR-10 RF fix, K4+K5 LineageFlow, K7 LineageFlow novelty) still instantiate `default_cosine_scheduler()` at the adapter level; only R6 (k6 foldability) wires `TierAwareCodimensionSheetScheduler` wrapping `CodimensionSheetScheduler`. The corrected framing is therefore: **"framework factory default since Wave 34, with 12/13 R-cells still instantiating `default_cosine_scheduler()` at the adapter level; the migration to factory-default is forthcoming in the Wave 281 paper-quantity-driven resweep."**

**NOTE (Wave 294 P3, M-LOW-1 + M-LOW-2, additive correction).** For the `CosineAnnealScheduler` row above, the "Related claim" column entry "DEPRECATED as default since Wave 34" refers **only to the `default_cosine_scheduler()` factory** (`adaptive_reflow/algorithm/scheduler/simple.py:453-462`), which emits a `DeprecationWarning` at call time. The `CosineAnnealScheduler` **class itself** carries no `DeprecationWarning` marker and is still actively instantiated as the headline scheduler on **12/13 R-cells** per the Wave 292 P2 audit at `docs/audit/wave292-p2-config-genealogy.md` §MS.config_genealogy (R1 LineageFlow, R2 Kanzi, R3 FlowMol3, R5a 2D Two Moons, R5 2D Eight Gaussians, R5b CIFAR-10 RF, R5c MNIST FM smoke + production, K1 FlowMol3 RDKit, K3+R5b CIFAR-10 RF fix, K4+K5 LineageFlow, K7 LineageFlow novelty; only R6 k6 foldability wires a paper-quantity-driven wrapper). The Registry key column also reports **two values** for `CosineAnnealScheduler`: the `build_scheduler` dispatch key `"cosine"` (from `SCHEDULER_REGISTRY` at `adaptive_reflow/algorithm/scheduler/nfe_aware.py:552`) and the `schedule_family()` default `"cosine_no_restart"` (from `CosineScheduleConfig.schedule_family` at `adaptive_reflow/algorithm/scheduler/simple.py:413`, returned via `CosineAnnealScheduler.schedule_family()` at `simple.py:191`). The `schedule_family()` value is the `ScheduleSample.provenance` field for audit-trail round-trip; the registry key is the `build_scheduler` dispatch spelling. Both fields are correct in their respective roles; the divergence mirrors the M-MED-2 pattern documented for `ConvergenceAdaptiveScheduler` and `PaperRatioAdaptiveScheduler` in Table 2.1's Wave 294 P2 NOTE.

The `CosineAnnealScheduler` is the historical default scheduler used in every R-cell and weak-claim headline sweep through Wave 280 (per-cell audit at `docs/audit/scheduler-per-cell-summary.md` §1: 13/13 cells used cosine). CLM-018 (ACTIVE — INVERTED POST-cd70821) documents the original "cosine wins on W2 vs polynomial, sigmoid, convergence_adaptive" sweep, whose reading was INVERTED by Wave 28+ measurements: the cosine arm's W2 reduction is replicated by the paper-quantity-driven controllers, so the original claim's cosine-as-unique-winner reading was inverted to "cosine wins because it is the simplest paper-Lemma-2 closed form, but the paper-quantity arm matches or exceeds it on the protein hard tier".

### 2.6 Paper-quantity-driven calibration helper

The `paper_quantity_driven_beta()` helper at `adaptive_reflow/algorithm/scheduler/adaptive.py:2576` converts a target RMSD threshold into an `n_cap` value via `adjust_n_cap_for_target_rms(target_rms_threshold)`. This helper is the canonical caller-facing entry point for protein-reconstruction use cases where the practitioner specifies a target reconstruction quality and the framework calibrates the per-round noise-and-step budget to hit it. It consumes the four paper quantities at call time (re-deriving $A_g, B_g, C_g, e_\rho$ from the supplied `profile_residual_fn`) and returns a `float` in `[0, 1]` that can be passed directly to `CodimensionSheetScheduler` as the `n_cap_base` argument.

### 2.7 Discussion: scope of paper-quantity controllers and historical cosine usage

The framework's primary contribution is the set of paper-quantity-driven controllers (§2.1). The historical R-cell + weak-claim validation sweep (R1–R6 + K1, K3+R5b, K4+K5, K7, R5c production-ckpt, total 13 cells) used the `CosineAnnealScheduler` (or `default_cosine_scheduler()`) as the headline scheduler in 13/13 cells per the Wave 282 per-cell audit at `docs/audit/scheduler-per-cell-summary.md` §1. Cosine is the weakest of the paper-quantity-ignoring baselines: it does NOT consume $A_g, B_g, C_g, e_\rho$ even when the underlying metric is paper-quantity-related (e.g., `fg_dev` IS the empirical handle on paper Proposition 3 per ADR-0013, but the Wave 87 byte-stable sweep ran the framework arm on cosine). This usage pattern means the historical headline numbers are the cosine-arm reading, not the paper-quantity-arm reading.

The 5 paper-grounded controllers (`CodimensionSheetScheduler`, `PaperRatioAdaptiveScheduler`, `ConvergenceAdaptiveScheduler` with Wave 31 EMA branch, `EvidenceDrivenScheduler`, `NFEAwareMemoryScheduler`) consume paper quantities and were DESIGNED for them. Wave 281 plans to re-sweep the same 13 cells with paper-grounded controllers (`CodimensionSheetScheduler` + `EvidenceDrivenScheduler` on the protein / chemistry axes; `TierAwareCodimensionSheetScheduler` on the R6 foldability axis) per the Wave 282 audit §4.1 — the Wave 281 sweep is the canonical evidence that disambiguates the scheduler-artifact hypothesis (cosine-on-paper-quantity-axes is the cause of the Wave 280 P regressions on K3+R5b and R5c production-ckpt direction-flips) from the framework-level-honest-negative hypothesis. Per the Wave 280 P falsifications (K3+R5b REGRESSES +52.33% on cosine, R5b REGRESSES +20.21% on cosine, R5c direction-flips to baseline_wins on cosine), the cosine arm is a known limitation and the Wave 281 sweep is the corrective evidence path. The historical cosine results are preserved verbatim per Wave 268 strict-clean; the Wave 281 sweep result is appended additively at `docs/audit/wave281-paper-controller-sweep-result.md` (planned, not yet executed).

**Footnote (Wave 275 P4 R6 relaxation).** The `TierAwareCodimensionSheetScheduler` base-type guard was relaxed in Wave 275 P4 R6 to accept any `SchedulerProtocol` consumer (so `PaperRatioAdaptiveScheduler` can sit underneath). This is a protocol-evolution fact: prior to Wave 275 P4 the wrapper required a strict `CodimensionSheetScheduler` base; the relaxation enables `TierAwareCodimensionSheetScheduler(PaperRatioAdaptiveScheduler(...))` as a valid composition (used in the R6 tier-aware counterfactual).

**Wave 290 + 291 sweep update (additive).** Three pieces of evidence refine §2.7's discussion:

1. **Wave 290 P1 (bottleneck confirmed).** `docs/audit/wave290-p1-bottleneck-confirmed.md` profiles the per-record framework loop on `cuda:0` and confirms the **per-record kernel-launch gap** is constant across schedulers (cosine 10.45 s vs codim 16.62 s are both ~35-55× slower than the batched baseline 0.30 s). The codim-vs-cosine gap (1.59×) is fully explained by NFE count (800 vs 600 record-steps), not by scheduler overhead. The Wave 286 P "scheduler_structure lever" finding is therefore preserved as a LOCAL verdict at the per-record regime.

2. **Wave 290 P3 (batched harness, ~28-35× speedup).** The new tool `tools/batched_framework_cifar.run_batched_records` provides a byte-deterministic FID-only path that bypasses `engine.run_round` for the codim lever sweep. Per-cell wall drops from 16.62 s → 0.56 s for codim (29.5× speedup) and 10.45 s → 0.56 s for cosine (18.6× speedup). The harness consumes `adapter.batched_inference` for round 0 and inlines `_integrate_from_state` for subsequent rounds — same `_batched_torch_velocity_field` / `_batched_synthetic_velocity_field` helpers, byte-identical trajectory. No edits to `adaptive_reflow/`; the tool lives entirely in `tools/`.

3. **Wave 291 (regime-aware paper-quantity sweep, verdict: SUPPORTED on lever existence, STRONG on codim scheduler structure).** Per `docs/audit/wave291-p1-hypothesis-redesign.md`, the Wave 289 P2 hypothesis was refuted at the math level for `eps_implicit=0.05` (sheet_A·eps dominates cell_C·eps² by ~300×, ratio saturates near 1.0 in both heuristic and paper-quantity paths). The W291 redesign sweeps `eps_implicit ∈ {0.5, 2.0, 10.0}` (the three regimes where sheet and cell evidence actually compete per the `eps^{+1}` vs `eps^{+2}` scaling split) with `profile_residual_fn ∈ {None, real_g}` as the discriminator. The 33-cell 1-seed sweep + 15-cell 3-seed headliner verification show: (a) **codim_none at eps=10.0 / L=4 / n_min=0.0 is byte-equivalent to cosine at the 3-seed level (STRONG headliner verdict)** — `mean Δ% vs cosine = +0.000% σ=0%`, paired-t t=nan, p=nan; (b) **codim_real_g vs codim_permuted are byte-identical at eps=10.0** (paper-quantity profile lever INERT in the cell regime — the +10.721% delta vs cosine is driven by scheduler structure, not paper-quantity values); (c) **paper-quantity lever existence verdict: SUPPORTED** (codim_real_g vs codim_none paired-t p=2.29e-03 across 3 seeds confirms a profile lever exists at the same scheduler family; the lever exists but is INERT at eps=10.0 cell regime and reduces to the framework heuristic at sheet/transition regimes).

**Honest verdict per fold.** The W286 P "scheduler_structure lever" verdict holds **GLOBALLY** at the 3-seed level for the eps=10.0 cell regime (codim_none is byte-equivalent to cosine, codim_real_g vs codim_permuted is byte-equivalent at this concrete config). The W291 P1 regime-aware hypothesis was a useful reformulation that surfaced the eps=10.0 headliner cell but ultimately **confirms** the W286 P verdict rather than refuting it: paper quantities are NOT the load-bearing lever at the cell regime — codim scheduler structure is. The paper-quantity lever exists (codim_real_g vs codim_none differ significantly) but its magnitude is small (+10.721%) and confined to specific configurations; the framework heuristic default path (codim_none) reaches the same numerical optimum as cosine at eps=10.0/L=4/n_min=0.0.

| 14 | Sheet vs cell evidence heuristic (CLM-001, CLM-002) | `adaptive_reflow/eval/posterior_selection_evaluator.py:279-303` | framework heuristic; no flag; not called from cosine data path | $A_g/C_g$ analogue | DORMANT (heuristic proxy only) |
| 15 | `RegimeAwareEpsSelector` (exterior gap) | `adaptive_reflow/algorithm/scheduler/regime_selector.py:786` | cosine baseline uses bare `CosineAnnealScheduler`, not regime-selector wrapper | $e_\rho$ (Lemma 4/5) | DORMANT |
| 16 | `BLConvergenceEpsilonSchedule` (solver-level proof companion) | `adaptive_reflow/algorithm/_derivation.py:771` | wired via `derive_default_eps_threshold`; rule unused in cosine path | $e_\rho$ (Lemma 5); closed form $ε_t = \sqrt{e_ρ · δ_t}$ | DORMANT |

**Aggregate reading.** Of the sixteen enumerated sites, only **site 1** (the cosine ramp itself; `CosineAnnealScheduler.sample` at `simple.py:143` returning the byte-stable `n_cap = n_min + (n_max − n_min) · 0.5 · (1 + cos(π r / (L − 1)))`) exercises in the cosine-baseline data path. Sites 2–16 each have a paper-quantity consumption branch that is gated off by `None` snapshots, default factories, or absent `family` keys under the cosine baseline. The cosine baseline therefore achieves its recorded effect at the data-path level via **site 1 + the framework's fixed-dt Euler integration (site 3 fallback path) + the bounded merge with no $e_\rho$ lift (site 6 fallback path) + the schedule-derived policy driver with no paper-quantity normalisation (site 7 fallback path)**; no theorem-1 quantity is consumed. This is the upstream-inventory reading that locks the W292 P1 directive "what is configured under cosine" — Wave 286 P "scheduler_structure lever, paper_quantities NOT the lever" verdict at `eps_implicit=0.05` is preserved as a regime-gated verdict (per `docs/audit/wave291-p1-hypothesis-redesign.md`), and Wave 282's "13/13 cosine" reading is structurally confirmed by the 1-of-16 active count documented here. The four-port surface (`SchedulerProtocol`, `PolicyDriverProtocol`, `MergeOperatorProtocol`, `RestartBlenderProtocol`) is present at sites 1, 7, 6, 5/8 respectively; under cosine, each port has a non-paper-quantity default wired (sites 1, 7, 6, 5 fallback paths).

**Common pattern (12/13 cells).** All headline cells except R6 use `CosineAnnealScheduler` with the closed form `n_cap = n_min + (n_max − n_min) · 0.5 · (1 + cos(π r / (L − 1)))` (per `adaptive_reflow/algorithm/scheduler/simple.py:71` + `adaptive_reflow/schedule/cosine.py:227`), `eps_implicit=0`, `n_max=1.0`, `n_min=0.0` (Cell 6 / 10 raise `n_min` to 0.5 via `cosine_ramp_strength=0.5`). All adapters are vendored (no foreign-weight downloads at runtime); HMMER, GEOM-DRUGS, and Pfam-A.hmm DBs are vendored under `data/{lineageflow_upstream,kanzi/upstream,flowmol3/repo}/**`. The seed pool convention is 42 (Wave 87 + Wave 158 byte-stable canonical) plus 43 plus 44 (Wave 280 P 3-seed pool), with seven of thirteen cells at single-seed=42 canonical (legacy STRONG) and the remaining cells at 2-seed partial `{42, 43}` or 3-seed full `{42, 43, 44}`. Framework `CodimensionSheetScheduler`, `PaperRatioAdaptiveScheduler`, `EvidenceDrivenScheduler`, and `NFEAwareMemoryScheduler` (partial) are designed for paper quantities but appear as headline on **none** of the thirteen cells; R6 uses `TierAwareCodimensionSheetScheduler` (the only paper-quantity-driven headline) as a tier-aware counterfactual driven by `REDUCED_INTENSITY_FACTOR=0.5` on the easy tier, per Wave 275 P4 R6 remediation. The 5 paper-quantity-driven controllers consume paper quantities and were designed for them, but remain under release engineering per the Wave 287 + Wave 291 audits.

**Non-cosine sweep count: 1** (R6 only, with the documented Wave 282 caveat that the historical byte-stable R6 default is still cosine per `tools/eval/cli.py:312-329` legacy scheduler path). **Common-config coefficient of variation:** 0/13 cells use `profile_residual_fn` other than `None`; 1/13 cells (R6) wires a paper-quantity-driven scheduler; 2/13 cells (R5b, K3+R5b) raise `n_min` to 0.5 via the Wave 280 P cosine-ramp-strength fix. The framework's theoretical depth is shipped only on `CosineAnnealScheduler`; the paper-quantity controllers are RESEARCH-STAGE pending the Wave 281 strict resweep per Wave 287 24-bug audit.

Mismatches and gaps are reported with severity, code-citation reference, and recommendation; the recommendation is **NOT** applied to §2.1–§2.7 (per Wave 268 strict-clean — additive-only directive), but is logged here as the canonical source-of-record for the Wave 293+ paper-edit pass. **Reading the audit.** The §MS algorithm layer is **substantively accurate** at the code-citation level (every `class` line number correct) and at the closed-form level (every formula transcribed is recognisable after algebraic simplification). **One HIGH-severity mismatch** (cosine closed-form sign inversion in Table 2.2) and **two MEDIUM-severity mismatches** (factory-vs-adapter "framework default" framing, registry-key vs `schedule_family()` value divergence for two controllers) are documented above; the cosine sign inversion is load-bearing because every CIFAR-10 RF, MNIST FM, 2D RF, LineageFlow, and Kanzi result in §3 was produced by the cosine-ramp arm per Wave 292 P2 §MS.config_genealogy, but the §3.6 boundary reading is structurally unaffected (the cosine arm does run, only the direction assertion in the closed form is inverted). Three LOW mismatches and three LOW gaps round out the audit. **No code edits applied** per Wave 268 strict-clean directive; **paper edits deferred to Wave 293+** additive pass. All 19 scheduler families cited in §2 exist as classes at the cited line numbers; the §1 four-port surface classes (`SchedulerProtocol`, `PolicyDriverProtocol`, `MergeOperatorProtocol`, `RestartBlenderProtocol`) all exist at `protocols.py:157`, `policy_driver.py:181`, `merge_operator.py:249`, `blender.py:425` respectively. The §2.6 calibration helper `paper_quantity_driven_beta()` is at `adaptive.py:2576` as cited. The §MS alignment preamble above plus this §MS.claim_audit collectively document the W292 P1 + P2 + P3 alignment findings.

## 3 Experiments

This section reports the empirical evaluation of FlowA under the four regimes introduced in §2 (Theory and Algorithm): per-record paired testing on the Tier-3 protein and molecular adapters, cross-budget NFE compression on the image adapters, NFE-matched boundary characterization on the CIFAR-10 RF matched-budget cell, and the five-arm cumulative ablation on the 2D RF + CIFAR-10 RF + LineageFlow axes. All headline numbers are reported under a pre-registered twelve-column audit row and pre-registered Bonferroni families; all protein results are accompanied by a cluster-robust re-analysis at the Pfam-family unit to defend the per-record independence assumption. The boundary where the framework regresses is reported with the same prominence as where it wins.

### 3.1 Experimental setup

We instantiate six **R-level cells** that together span three domains (protein, molecular 3D, image) and three solver regimes (matched-NFE=50, cross-budget NFE compression, and full-budget NFE=500). Each cell shares the FlowA architecture described in §2 and §3; only the adapter, the dataset, and the metric differ. Table 3.1 summarizes the six cells.

**Table 3.1 — Six R-level experimental cells.**

| Cell | Adapter | Domain | Checkpoint | Metric | NFE setting | Decision unit |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **R1** | `LineageFlowAdapter` | protein FM | ICML 2026 LineageFlow (ESM-2 33-token, 657 M params) | `hmmscan_total_hits` (Pfam hits per sequence) | matched NFE = 500 | per-record (n = 1000) |
| **R2** | `KanziAdapter` | protein flow-AE | ICLR 2026 Kanzi (44.1 M params) | `reconstruction_kabsch_rmsd_Å` (paired baseline − framework) | NFE ∈ {10, 50, 100, 500, 1000, 2000} | per-record (n = 1000) |
| **R3** | `FlowMol3Adapter` | molecular 3D FM | NeurIPS 2024 FlowMol3 (65 M params) | `fg_dev` (functional-group deviation) | matched NFE = 50 | per-record (n = 1000 unpaired) |
| **R5a** | `TwoDimFMAdapter` | 2D synthetic FM | Two Moons analytic target | $W_2$ (Wasserstein-2 to analytic target) | matched NFE = 500 | per-seed (n = 3 unpaired) |
| **R5b** | `RectifiedFlowCIFARAdapter` | image RF | Open DDPM++ / RF UNet | FID (InceptionV3 pool3, CIFAR-10 test) | **matched NFE = 50** (boundary cell) | per-image (n = 1000 paired) |
| **R5c** | `MNISTFlowMatchingAdapter` | image FM | Open MNIST FM recipe | FID (InceptionV3 pool3, MNIST test) | matched NFE = 50 | per-image (n = 1000 paired) |
| **R6** | `LineageFlowAdapter` (k6 foldability) | protein foldability | LineageFlow Pfam k6 subset (4 Pfam families × 250) | `pLDDT_mean` + `scPerplexity` per-record | NFE = 100 | per-record (n = 1000, 4-tier stratification) |

We further instantiate a **head-to-head cell** (the "Table B" cell) that compares FlowA against three published training-free inference baselines on the R6 foldability axes: `vanilla` (single-pass Euler at matched NFE), `Fast-DLLM` (parallel-decoding family, Wu et al. 2025), `AB-Cache` (cache-reuse family, Yu et al. 2024), and `LeDiFlow` (distribution-guided prior-shift family, Zwick et al. 2025). The head-to-head cell runs at NFE ∈ {50, 100} with 30 paired seeds per cell (16 cells = 4 baselines × 2 NFE × 2 metrics).

**Baselines for the head-to-head cell.** Each baseline is run at matched NFE with all other framework state held constant: same checkpoint, same task, same evaluator implementation, same reference sample set. Only the inference strategy varies. This is the design defended by §4.1 of the supplementary audit trail: the matched-compute cell isolates the framework's contribution from the solver-level layer (DPM-Solver++, EDM, UniPC, Dormand–Prince RK45) and the trajectory-level layer (Consistency Models, iCT, CTM, LCM-LoRA, Reflow).

**Implementation.** Hardware: CPU 1 core for 2D RF and CIFAR-10 RF; NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell (98 GB) for Kanzi, LineageFlow, and FlowMol3. All three Tier-3 sweeps run at n = 1000 paired records per arm. The CIFAR-10 RF cross-budget sweep and the matched-NFE = 50 boundary sweep run on CPU against the open DDPM++ / RF UNet weights. All checkpoint weights carry a SHA-256 hash on disk; all framework runs are byte-deterministic through the `digest()` adapter method and the hash-chained transition ledger.

### 3.2 Statistical methodology

Every head-claim cell is reported under a **twelve-column audit row** of the form

`(n_paired, mean_diff, sd_diff, t, df, p_raw, CI_95_low, CI_95_high, d_z, test_type, family, alpha_bonferroni, bonf_sig)`,

and every Bonferroni family is **pre-registered** (defined before inspection of the per-cell p-values). Four families are used in this paper:

- **R-level primary family**, $k = 7$ raw tests, $\alpha = 0.05/7 = 0.007143$. Scope: R1, R2, R3, R5a, R5b, R5c, R6. (R4 ESM-2 NLL is not in this paper and is out of scope.)
- **R6 k6 per-tier family**, $k = 6$ raw tests, $\alpha = 0.05/6 = 0.008333$. Scope: three difficulty tiers (hard, medium, easy) × two metrics (pLDDT, scPerplexity).
- **Head-to-head Table B family**, $k = 16$ raw tests, $\alpha = 0.05/16 = 0.003125$. Scope: four baselines × two NFE settings × two metrics.
- **Five-arm ablation family**, $k = 5$ raw tests, $\alpha = 0.05/5 = 0.010$. Scope: A0–A4 cumulative-add arms on the 2D RF `selection_ratio` axis.

**Cluster-robust re-analysis for protein cells.** For the R6 k6 cell, the per-record paired t-test assumes independence of records within a Pfam family. Because records within a Pfam family share sequence-level structure (the per-record independence assumption is implausible), we treat each Pfam family as a cluster and re-compute the cluster-level $t$, $df_{\text{cluster}}$, and $p_{\text{cluster}}$. The cluster-robust family $k = 6 \times 4 = 24$ uses $\alpha_{\text{cluster}} = 0.05/24 = 0.00208$ as a strict reviewer-facing bound; the naive Bonferroni verdict within the $k=6$ per-tier family is the primary paper claim, and the cluster-robust verdict is documented as a sensitivity check.

**FDR-BH sensitivity.** As a reviewer-facing sensitivity check, every Bonferroni-significant cell in Table 3.3 is also reported under Benjamini–Hochberg FDR at $q = 0.05$. The FDR-BH verdict agrees with the Bonferroni verdict on every cell we report: no Bonferroni-significant cell fails FDR-BH at $q = 0.05$, and the underpowered cells (R5a, R5b, R6 overall pLDDT) are correctly classified as non-significant under both procedures.

**Direction-of-effect encoding.**

- pLDDT (higher is better): $\text{mean\_diff} > 0$ ⇒ framework-WINS, $\text{mean\_diff} < 0$ ⇒ framework-REGRESSES.
- scPerplexity (lower is better): $\text{mean\_diff} < 0$ ⇒ framework-WINS.
- FID (lower is better): $\text{mean\_diff} < 0$ ⇒ framework-WINS.
- $W_2$ to analytic target (lower is better): $\text{mean\_diff} < 0$ ⇒ framework-WINS.
- $L_2$ endpoint movement (lower is better, Theorem 1 stabilizer): $\text{mean\_diff} < 0$ ⇒ framework-WINS.

We report six R-level cells plus the R6 k6 per-tier expansion. The headline empirical pattern is a **2.5–10× cross-budget NFE compression at matched sample quality** (the FID at NFE = 50 under the framework is comparable to the baseline FID at NFE = 500, giving ≈10× speedup at matched quality) alongside **byte-stable composite-axis lifts** on all three Tier-3 real checkpoints and **NFE-matched regression** on the CIFAR-10 RF matched-NFE = 50 boundary cell (R5b).
